**Osaka Iron Works, K.K** Dojima Hama-dori, Kita-ku, Osaka. Est. 1904; cap. ¥12,000 (10,500 p.u.): Shipbuilding and machinery making. Pres. E. Nakayama — p. 145 ([scan leaf 924](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n924/mode/1up))

**Oshima Steel Foundry, K.K** Oshimacho, Minami-katsushika, Tokyo. Taken over by Messrs. H. Ogawa, H. Tanaka, and H. Okura, the Dirs. for ¥2,700 in 1917 and reformed with expanded cap. ¥3,570 p.u. — p. 145 ([scan leaf 924](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n924/mode/1up))

**Sumitomo Firm** Osaka, one of the oldest millionaire houses in Japan, combining banking, mining, refining, electric wire dep'ts, etc., owns Besshi Copper Mine, etc., and run copper works, electric wire and cable works, both at Osaka, fertilizer factory at Niihama, Ehime-ken; manufactures steel ingots, steel forgings, etc. for railways, shipbuilding yards, etc. Pres. Baron Sumitomo; Mng.-Dir. K. Nakada. — p. 145 ([scan leaf 924](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n924/mode/1up))

**Takata Mining Co., K.K** Yurakucho, 2-chome, Koj., Tokyo. Founded in 1918 by the late Mr. S. Takata, a millionaire merchant; cap. ¥10,000 (4,500 p.u.); besides mining, attend to refinery and chemical industry; also manufacture and deal in various hardware, tools, and machines. Pres. Kanukichi Takata, Dirs. T. Ikeda, G. Taguchi, etc. (Vide Sect. General). — p. 145 ([scan leaf 924](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n924/mode/1up))

**Tokyo Kozai, (Steel-materials), Co., K.K** Oshima-mura, Tokyo, est. '12, cap. ¥3,000 p.u. Pres. T. Haynshiyn. — p. 145 ([scan leaf 924](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n924/mode/1up))

**Toyo Iron Works, K.K** Zenikame-cho, Koj., Tokyo. Est. 1917 under the Govt. protection and with cap. ¥34,000 p.u. Pres. Baron S Go, Mng.-Dirs. Baron K. Nakajima, etc. — p. 145 ([scan leaf 924](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n924/mode/1up))

**Yamashita Mining, K.K** Gofuku-cho, Nih., Tokyo. Est. 1918; cap. 20,000 (½ p.u.) combined with Yamashita S.S. Co. in '20. Pres. K. Yamashita. — p. 145 ([scan leaf 924](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n924/mode/1up))


## The Japan Year Book

### Section IX. Brewery

**Dai Nippon Beer Brewery Co., K.K** Meguro, Tokyo. Formed March, 1906 by amalgamating Ebisu, Sapporo and Asahi breweries. The foremost Co. in this line; annual products 470,000 koku. Brands "Ebisu," "Sapporo," "Asahi," and "Munchonner" beers; Pres. K. Makoshi; Mng.-Dir. C. Uemura. — p. 146 ([scan leaf 925](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n925/mode/1up))

**Eigashima Saké Brewery Co., K.K** Okubo, Akashi, Hyogo-ken Est. June 1888; annual products 15,000 koku. Directors: Pres. Kyokichi Tobe; Mng. Toyotaro Tobe. — p. 146 ([scan leaf 925](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n925/mode/1up))

**Kabuto Beer Brewery Co., K.K** Ginza, Tokyo. Est. Sept. '96; Brand, "Kabuto."; reorganized in '21 amalgamating in May, the Teikoku Mineral Water Co. and the Nippon Bottle Mfg. Co., and renamed Nippon Beer, Kosen Co. K.K. Pres. K. Nezu, Mng.-Dir. T. Kameda. — p. 146 ([scan leaf 925](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n925/mode/1up))

**Kirin Beer Brewery Co., K.K** Bund, Yokohama. Founded by some foreigners and reorganized in Feb. 1907 as K.K. with Japanese and foreign share-holders; brands, "Lager," "Pilsena," etc. Cap. ¥5,000 : annual products 140,000 koku. Pres S. Ida. — p. 146 ([scan leaf 925](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n925/mode/1up))

**Kondo Rihei and Co., K.K** No. 9, 2-chome, Honcho, Nihonbashi, Tokyo. Est. 1918; cap. ¥1,000 p.u.; dealers in Bee Brand Kuzan wine, Ushiku wine and other liquors. Pres. R. Kondo. — p. 146 ([scan leaf 925](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n925/mode/1up))

**Nichi-Ei (Anglo-Jap.) Brewery Co., K.K** Marunouchi, Tokyo. Est. '20; cap. ¥[illegible]0,000 p.u.; Brand "Cascade." Pres T. Kusumi. — p. 146 ([scan leaf 925](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n925/mode/1up))

**Nishinomiya Saké Brewery Co., K.K** Nishinomiya, Hyogo. Est. Apr. 1889; annual products 24,000 koku. — p. 146 ([scan leaf 925](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n925/mode/1up))

**G. Tanaka & Co** Choshi, Chiba-ken. Est. 1616, one of the oldest breweries of Japanese soy. Brand, "Higeta Shoyu"; have been honored with medals at exhibitions at home and abroad. Prop. G. Tanaka. — p. 146 ([scan leaf 925](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n925/mode/1up))

**Teikoku Beer Brewery Co., K.K** Dairi, Fukuoka-ken. Est. May, 1912. Have capacity to produce 65,000 koku a year. Pres. I. Sumida, Dirs. T. Hirataka, M. Miyamoto, etc. — p. 146 ([scan leaf 925](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n925/mode/1up))

**Teikoku Mineral Water Co., K.K** Hirano, near Kobe; and Sakamoto-cho, Nihonbashi, Tokyo. Est. Jan. 1907; own Mitsuya & Peacock brands of Hirano table water containing natural carbonic acid gas, obtained from a spring at Hirano village, near Ikeda station of the Hankaku railway. (Vide Kabuto Beer). — p. 146 ([scan leaf 925](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n925/mode/1up))

### Section X. Sugar Manufacturing

**Dai Nippon Sugar Mfg. Co., K.K** Suna-mura, Minami-Katsushika-gun, Tokyo-fu. Est. 1896; capital ¥12,000 p.u.; factories at Tokyo, Osaka, Moji, Formosa producing 300,000 piculs of crude sugar in 1921. Pres. E. Fujiyama; Mng.-Dirs. C. Takayama, etc. — p. 146 ([scan leaf 925](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n925/mode/1up))

**Ensuiko Sugar Mfg. and Colonization Co., K.K** Kagi, Formosa Est. 1907; capital ¥17,750 p.u.; manufactured 500,000 piculs of crude sugar in '21. The Co. amalgamated in 1914 with Taito Colonization Co. (est. Aug., 1912; cap. ¥2,250 p.u.) Pres. T. Maki; Mng.-Dirs. S. Fujisaki, etc. — p. 146 ([scan leaf 925](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n925/mode/1up))


## Business Directory

**Meiji Sugar Mfg. Co., K.K** Tainan, Formosa. Est 1906; capital increased to ¥32,500 (¥11,425 p.u.) in 1921 with the amalgamation of the Yamato Sugar Co., enlarging its capacity to 560,000 piculs of crude sugar in 7 factories in Fukuoka, Kanagawa, and Formosa. Has connection with Tokyo Confectionary Co., (Cap. ¥2,000) and Boso Condensed Milk Co. (cap. ¥1,100). Chairman H. Soma; Mng.-Dirs. K. Arima, H. Chiba. — p. 147 ([scan leaf 926](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n926/mode/1up))

**Nan-Man (South Manchuria) Sugar Mfg. Co., K.K** Japanese Concession, Mukden, China. Est. 1916; cap. ¥10,000 (½ p.u.) Pres. T. Arai, Mng.-Dir. J. Shiraishi. — p. 147 ([scan leaf 926](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n926/mode/1up))

**Nanyo (South-Sea) Sugar Mfg. Co., K.K** 1-chome, Yurakucho, Koj., Tokyo. Est. 1918; cap. ¥2,400 p.u. Dir. K. Akimoto (chairman). — p. 147 ([scan leaf 926](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n926/mode/1up))

**Niitaka Sugar Mfg. Co., K.K** Sentoho, Taichu, Formosa. Est Oct. 1909; capital ¥10,750; capacity 300,000 piculs of crude sugar in '21. Pres. K. Takashima; Mng.-Dir. K. Makiyama. — p. 147 ([scan leaf 926](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n926/mode/1up))

**Tainan Sugar Mfg. Co., K.K** Tainan, Formosa. Est. Nov., 1912; cap. ¥20,250 (17,000 p.u.) The Co. amalgamated in 1916 with Antai Co. and Giran Co. Pres. U. Suzuki; Mng.-Dirs. K. Kawakami, etc. — p. 147 ([scan leaf 926](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n926/mode/1up))

**Taiwan Sugar Mfg. Co., K.K** Takao, Formosa. Formed 1900; capital ¥63,000 (38,100 p.u.) gradually enlarged its capacity with amalgamation with minor cos since the war, about 1,000,000 piculs in '21. Chairman S. Fujita; Mng.-Dirs. T. Yamamoto, N. Takechi, T. Masuda. — p. 147 ([scan leaf 926](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n926/mode/1up))

**Teikoku Sugar Mfg. Co., K.K** Taichu, Formosa. Est. 1910; cap. ¥15,000 p.u.; produced 450,000 piculs of crude sugar in '21 The Co. amalgamated in '16 with Minami-Nippon Sugar Mfg. Co. (cap. ¥2,500 p.u.) Pres. M. Matsukata; Mng.-Dir. S. Makiyama. — p. 147 ([scan leaf 926](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n926/mode/1up))

**Toyo Sugar Mfg. Co., K.K** Kagi, Formosa. Est. 1907; absorbed several Cos in the last decade with the expanded capital (¥12,550 in '21) and capacity, the latter being put at 600,000 piculs of crude sugar besides 8,000 koku of alcohol, in 6 factories in Formosa and Okinawa. Pres. T. Shimosaka, Mng.-Dir. R. Hinata, T. Tamura. — p. 147 ([scan leaf 926](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n926/mode/1up))

### Section XI. Flour Milling and Confectionery

**Imamura Confectioners, K.K** No. 2, Mita-Koyamacho, Shiba, Tokyo. Est. 1918; cap. ¥1,000 (¼ p.u.) Pres. T. Imamura. — p. 147 ([scan leaf 926](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n926/mode/1up))

**Manchuria Flour Milling Co., K.K** Tieling, S. Manchuria. Est. 1908; cap. ¥3,202 p.u.; manufacture 1,200 barrels per day; have branches at Changchun, Harbin and Tainan. Pres. T. Yoshimura. — p. 147 ([scan leaf 926](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n926/mode/1up))

**Masuda Flour Mill, K.K** Higashi-shiri-Ikenuma, Kobe. Est. 1908; a day's capacity is 1,000 barrels. Pres. F. Nakamura, Dir. E. Masuda, etc. — p. 147 ([scan leaf 926](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n926/mode/1up))


## The Japan Year Book

**Morinaga & Co., K.K** confectioners, Mita, Tokyo. Est. in 191[illegible] succeeding Mr. Morinaga's confectionery business, with capital ¥[illegible] (900 p.u.); Directors, T. Morinaga (Pres.), H. Matsuzaki (Gen.-Mgr.), T. Kakujima (Mng. of Osaka Branch). — p. 148 ([scan leaf 927](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n927/mode/1up))

**Nippon Flour Milling Co., K.K** Yayesu-cho, Koj., Tokyo. Est. Sept. 1896. Cap. ¥11,550, capacity in 24 hours put at 1,800 barrels in '22. at its 3 shops. Pres. S. Iwasaki, Vice.-Pres. M. Hirano. — p. 148 ([scan leaf 927](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n927/mode/1up))

**Nisshin Flour Milling Co., K.K** Suyehirogashi, Nihombashi, Tokyo. Est. Mar. '07. Capacity 1,800 barrels in 24 hrs in '21. Pres. I Nezu; Mng.-Dir. T. Shota. — p. 148 ([scan leaf 927](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n927/mode/1up))

**To-a Flour Milling Co., K.K** Oshimacho, suburb of Tokyo. Est. Oct. 1906. Cap. ¥1,250 p.u.; capacity 700 barrels. Mng.-Dir. Moroi. — p. 148 ([scan leaf 927](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n927/mode/1up))

### Section Xii. Paper And Paper Wares

**Chosen Paper-Mill Co., K.K** New Wiju, Korea. Est. Dec 1917; cap. ¥5,000 (¼ p.u.), sister-Co. of the Oji Paper-Mill, Tokyo. Pres. G. Fujiwara; Dir. T. Hara. — p. 148 ([scan leaf 927](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n927/mode/1up))

**Fuji Paper-Mill Co., K.K** Sanjikkembori, Kyobashi, Tokyo. Est. 1887; owns 10 mills in Shizuoka-ken, Hokkaido & Osaka; 3 branches in China; annual product 100 million lbs. Pres. H. Okawa; Mng.-Dirs. Y. Anamizu, T. Takahashi, etc. — p. 148 ([scan leaf 927](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n927/mode/1up))

**Fuji Pulp Mfg. Co., K.K** Sanjikkenbori, Kyobashi, Tokyo. Est. Oct. '18; cap. ¥3,000 p.u.; has connection with the Fuji Paper Mill. Chairman, Y. Anamizu. — p. 148 ([scan leaf 927](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n927/mode/1up))

**Japan Chemical Pulp Mfg. Co., K.K** Sakuhama, Karafuto. Est. 1915; cap. ¥3,000 p.u. Tokyo Branch, No. 21 Mitsubishi building, Yaesucho, Kojimachi. Pres. K. Koike; Dirs. K. Tsukakoshi, S. Arita. — p. 148 ([scan leaf 927](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n927/mode/1up))

**Karafuto Industrial Co., K.K** Tomarie-machi. Karafuto. Est. 1913; cap. ¥11,000 (7,700 p.u.); engaged in manufacture of pulp and also in supply of electric power. Pres. H. Okawa; Dirs. E. Tanaka, etc. — p. 148 ([scan leaf 927](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n927/mode/1up))

**Karafuto Sangyo Kabushiki Kaisha** Otomari. Karafuto. Manufactures of and dealers in pulp and foreign paper. Est. July '18; cap. ¥10,000 (6,250 p.u.); supply pulp to the Oji Paper-Mill. Pres. G. Fujiwara. — p. 148 ([scan leaf 927](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n927/mode/1up))

**Kashiwabara Paper Store, K.K** 1-chome, Minami-Demma-cho, Kyobashi, Tokyo. Est. 1916; cap. ¥1,000; dealers in pulp, foreign and native papers; importers and exporters; a branch at Osaka. Pres. M. Kashiwabara, Dirs. K. Tsunoda, etc., Man. K. Ito. — p. 148 ([scan leaf 927](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n927/mode/1up))

**Mitsubishi Paper-Mill Co., K.K** Takasago, Hyogo-ken. Founded and run by the Iwasaki family as gmk.; reformed in 1918 as such; cap. ¥2,500. Pres. T. Tawara, Dirs. K. Kimura, E. Fujino. — p. 148 ([scan leaf 927](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n927/mode/1up))

**Nakanoshima Paper-Mill Co., K.K** Tamacho, Kitaku, Osaka. Est. Oct. '06; cap. ¥1,500.; annual prod., match-paper 2 million lbs and packing paper 16 million lbs. Pres. Dembei Shimogo; Mng.-Dir. T. Shimogo; Mng. M. Ogawa. — p. 148 ([scan leaf 927](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n927/mode/1up))


## Business Directory

**Oji Paper Mfg. Co., K.K** General offices; 9 Shinsaiwa'-cho, Shiba, Tokyo. Founded in 1873. Cap. p.u. ¥31,500; res. 10,000. Mills at Tomakomai and 9 other places. Subsidiary concerns:- Chosen Paper Mfg. Co., Karafuto Industrial Co., etc. Annual Output: Paper—100,000 tons; Sulphite Pulp—30,000 tons, etc. Pres. G. Fujiwara; Dirs. K. Takashima, etc. — p. 149 ([scan leaf 928](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n928/mode/1up))

**Okayama Paper-Mill Co., K.K** Okayama. Est. Jan, '03; cap. ¥625 p.u.; export paper to China, Korea, Australia, South Seas and India. Mng.-Dir. T. Mizuta, T. Uyeda, K. Umeda, J. Nakamura. — p. 149 ([scan leaf 928](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n928/mode/1up))

**Tokai Pulp Mfg Co., K.K** Yariyncho, Kyobashiku, Tokyo. Est. 1907. Pres. K. Okura; Dirs. Y. Haraguchi, H. Okura. — p. 149 ([scan leaf 928](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n928/mode/1up))

**Toyo Paper-Mill Co., K.K** Toyosaki-machi, Osaka-fu. Est. 1906; manufacture rice paper for cigarette. Pres. S. Inouye; Mng.-Dir. T. Nakamura. — p. 149 ([scan leaf 928](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n928/mode/1up))

### Section XIII. Glass and Ceramics

**Asahi Glass Mfg. Co., K.K** 1-chome, Eirakucho, Koj., Tokyo. Est. 1909; cap. ¥12,500; manufacturers of plate glass; run by the Iwasaki family; branch-shops at Amagasaki, Hyogo-ken, etc. Pres. T. Iwasaki. Mng.-Dir. H. Tamura, S. Yamada. — p. 149 ([scan leaf 928](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n928/mode/1up))

**Nikka (Japan-China) Ceramic Industry Co., K.K** Tsi-nan-fu, Shantung, China. Est. 1918; cap. ¥2,000; besides manufacturing earthen ware, engaged in coal mining and supply of electricity. Dirs. Y. Nakamura, E. Watanabe. — p. 149 ([scan leaf 928](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n928/mode/1up))

**Osaka Ceramic Co., K.K** Dojima, Kitaku, Osaka. Est. 1822; cap. ¥7,000 p.u.; produce sundry varieties of bricks including ordinary bricks at the factories at Sakai, Kishiwada, Kaizuka, etc. Pres. R. Isono; Dir. H. Shirazaki. — p. 149 ([scan leaf 928](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n928/mode/1up))

**Shinagawa White Brick Mfg. Co., K.K** Kita-Shinagawa, Tokyo-fu. Est. 1875 and organized as K.K. in 1903; cap. ¥3,500, yearly output estimated at 30,000,000 fire-proof, and 15,000,000 decorative; have a factory at Osaka and 3 at Fukuoka prefecture. Dirs. M. Yamauchi, Y. Fujimura. — p. 149 ([scan leaf 928](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n928/mode/1up))

**Shofu Porcelain Co., K.K** Fukakusa near Kyoto. Est. about 1861 and organized as such in 1906; manufacturers and wholesale exporters of Japanese crockery wares; have a large factory in Nagoya to produce export wares, and sale office at Kyobashiku, Tokyo; export to China, India, South Seas and Australia. Prop. Kajo Shofu. — p. 149 ([scan leaf 928](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n928/mode/1up))

**Aichi Cement Mfg. Co., K.K** Atsuta-Higashi-cho, Minami-ku, Nagoya. Est. 1889; cap. ¥1,650 p.u. Pres. Takashima. — p. 149 ([scan leaf 928](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n928/mode/1up))

### Printing and Publishing Houses

**Hakubun-Kan** publishing house, Honkoku-cho, Nihombashi, Tokyo, founded 1887 by the late Sahei Ohashi, father of the present proprietor, Shintaro Ohashi. Issues 15 kinds of magazines, etc. — p. 154 ([scan leaf 933](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n933/mode/1up))

**Kaisei-Kan** Kobinata-Suidocho, Koishikawa, Tokyo. A leading publisher of text-books for schools; publishes Westernized Japanese song books. Prop. T. Nishino — p. 154 ([scan leaf 933](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n933/mode/1up))

**Kyobun-Kan** (Methodist Publishing House), Ginza, Tokyo. Leading book-sellers and stationers in Tokyo, also undertake printing. Agents, Ginn & Co., Boston. (Gen.-Mng.) E. J. Igrehart — p. 154 ([scan leaf 933](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n933/mode/1up))

**Maruzen Kabushiki Kaisha** Publishers, book-sellers and stationers. Tori-Sanchome, Nihombashi, Tokyo. Est. 1880, capital ¥1,300 p.u. in '20; connections, world wide. Dirs. N. Yamazaki, R. Matsushita, etc. Branches; Osaka, Kyoto, Sendai, and Fukuoka, Sapporo. — p. 154 ([scan leaf 933](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n933/mode/1up))

**Nisshin Insatsu, Ltd** Yamabuki-cho, Ushigome, Tokyo. Est. 1909. General printing; cap. ¥500. Mng.-Dir. S. Kokuyo. — p. 154 ([scan leaf 933](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n933/mode/1up))

**Shuyeisha Pub. Co., Ltd** Nishi Konya-cho, Ky., Tokyo. Est. 1899. General printing; cap. ¥1,000. Mng.-Dir. Y. Sugiyama. — p. 154 ([scan leaf 933](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n933/mode/1up))

**Tokyo Printing Co., K.K** Printer. Kabutocho, Nihombashi, Tokyo. Capital ¥237 p.u.; reserves ¥144; contractors to several Government Departments. Awarded gold medals at home exhibitions. Mng.-Dir. Z. Hoshino. — p. 154 ([scan leaf 933](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n933/mode/1up))

**Osaka Shosen Kaisha, K.K** Tomishimacho, N., Osaka. Cap. ¥100,000 ; res. ¥17,323. Pres. K. Hori; Vice-Pres. R. Kafuku; Dirs. R. Fukao, etc. — p. 156 ([scan leaf 935](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n935/mode/1up))

**Tokyo Bay S.S. Co., K.K** Reigunjima, Kyobashi, Tokyo. Est. 1899 ; cap. ¥2,000 (1,400 p.u.): Mng.-Dir. K. Sakurai. — p. 156 ([scan leaf 935](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n935/mode/1up))

**Toyo Kisen Kaisha, K.K** Yurakucho, Koj., Tokyo. Pres. S. Asano; Dirs. H. Okawa, K. Okura, M. Shiraishi, T. Ito, R. Asano, etc. — p. 156 ([scan leaf 935](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n935/mode/1up))

**Uchida S S. Co., K.K** Kobe. Est. 1916; cap. ¥10,000 p.u. Pres S. Uchida, Mng.-Dir. T. Kasahara. — p. 156 ([scan leaf 935](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n935/mode/1up))

### Section XXI. Shipbuilding

**Asano Shipbuilding Co., K.K** Hashimoto-cho, Yamanuchi-cho, Yhama. Founded April 1917 by Mr. Soichiro Asano, President ; cap. ¥3,750 p.u. ; can build 30,000 ton class steamers. — p. 156 ([scan leaf 935](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n935/mode/1up))

**Hakodate Dockyard Co., K.K** Hakodate, Hokkaido. Est. Nov., 1896 ; cap. ¥4,000 (3,200 p.u.); reserves ¥600 ; Pres. T. Kawata ; Mng.-Dir. K. Kondo. — p. 156 ([scan leaf 935](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n935/mode/1up))

**Ishikawajima Dockyards, K.K** Ishikawajima, Tokyo. Shipbuilding and Engineering works: one of the oldest private establishments; reorganized in Jan. 1887 as K.K. with expanded business; capital ¥5,000 p.u. Pres. Watanabe; Mng. Uchida. — p. 156 ([scan leaf 935](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n935/mode/1up))

**Kawasaki Dockyard Co., K.K** Kobe; founded in 1878 by Mr. Shozo Kawasaki; reorganized in '96 as K. K.; cap. ¥56,250 p.u. Started 1917 marine transportation business and its iron works newly built at suburbs of Kobe have begun to mfr. iron plate. Pres. Kojiro Matsukata. Dirs. G. Nomoto, etc. — p. 156 ([scan leaf 935](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n935/mode/1up))

**Mitsubishi Dockyards** established by the Mitsubishi Firm; was reorganized in 1918 as an independent joint stock company with the nominal capital of ¥50,000; (see the Mitsubishi Firm under Sec. General). — p. 156 ([scan leaf 935](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n935/mode/1up))

**Osaka Iron Works, K.K** Kawaguchi, Osaka. Est. March, 1914 as K.K. Pres. J. Yamaoka; Mng.-Dirs. R. Yamaguchi; K. Kimura. — p. 156 ([scan leaf 935](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n935/mode/1up))

**Osaka Shipbuilding Co., K.K** Sembi-cho, Nishi-ku, Osaka. Est. 1916; cap. ¥2,500 p.u. Pres. N. Iwaki. — p. 156 ([scan leaf 935](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n935/mode/1up))

**Uraga Dockyard Co., K.K** Kinkuchō, Kojimachi, Tokyo. Est 1896; cap. ¥10,000 (6,250 p.u.) Pres. K. Yamashita. Mng.-Dir. J Imaoka. — p. 156 ([scan leaf 935](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n935/mode/1up))

**Yokohama Dockyard Co., K.K** Nagasumi-cho, Yokohama. Est. June, 1891; cap. ¥10,000 p.u.; Chairman of Dirs. T. Suda; Mng.-Dirs M Miyanaga, etc. — p. 156 ([scan leaf 935](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n935/mode/1up))


## Business Directory

### Section XXII. Hotels

**Chosen Hotel** Keijo (Seoul), Korea. Opened in Oct. 1914; the ground on which its fire-proof building stands is said to have been formerly a part of the "Temple of Heaven"; cable add. "Ch[illegible]," Keijo. Mng. S. Inohara. — p. 157 ([scan leaf 936](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n936/mode/1up))

**Fujiya Hotel, K.K** Miyanoshita, Hakone. Formed '93 by the Yamaguchi family; is reputed as one of the best hotels in Japan, and is provided with hot spring bath. — p. 157 ([scan leaf 936](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n936/mode/1up))

**Grand Hotel** Yokohama. commands an unbroken view of surrounding country. Mng.-Dir. H. E. Manwaring. — p. 157 ([scan leaf 936](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n936/mode/1up))

**Imperial Hotel, K.K** Uchi-Yamashita-cho, Kojimachi, Tokyo. Est. in 1907; cap. ¥2,400; Directors, K. Okura (Pres.) — p. 157 ([scan leaf 936](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n936/mode/1up))

**Kanaya Hotel** Nikko. 15 minutes walk from Nikko Station; situated near the sacred bridge. Proprietor, Kanaya. — p. 157 ([scan leaf 936](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n936/mode/1up))

**Mikasa Hotel** Karuizawa, Nagano-ken. Open May—Oct. in the famous summer resort, 4½ hrs. from Tokyo. Asama can be ascended from Karuizawa. Mng. S. Matsuda. — p. 157 ([scan leaf 936](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n936/mode/1up))

**Miyako Hotel** Kyoto, has 150 bed rooms and fine walks. Mng. M. Hamanguchi. — p. 157 ([scan leaf 936](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n936/mode/1up))

**Nara Hotel** Nara near Kyoto. Under direct management of Govt. Rlys.; built on sunny, lofty hill in Deer Park in palace style. — p. 157 ([scan leaf 936](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n936/mode/1up))

**Nikko Hotel** Nikko. Situated at the adjoining ground to the temples and shrines. Prop. H. Arai. — p. 157 ([scan leaf 936](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n936/mode/1up))

**Oriental Hotel** Kobe. Purchased in 1917 by Toyo Kisen Kaisha and operated by the Co. in connection with their steamship service. Mng. W. Clark. — p. 157 ([scan leaf 936](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n936/mode/1up))

**Sanyo Hotel** Shimonoseki. Under direct management of Govt. Rlys.; on station premises. — p. 157 ([scan leaf 936](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n936/mode/1up))

**Station Hotel** at Tokyo Station. Under direct control of Gov. Rlys. — p. 157 ([scan leaf 936](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n936/mode/1up))

**Tsukiji-Seiyokan Hotel** Tokyo. Est. 1869 and rebuilt in 1911. Mng. Y. Seno. — p. 157 ([scan leaf 936](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n936/mode/1up))

**Yamato Hotels** at Changchun (Terminus of South Manchuria, Chinese Eastern, and Kirin-Changchun Rlys., Mukden, Port Arthur, Dairen, and Hoshigaura (the finest summer resort in North China) near Dairen. Run on European line under the direct management of the S.M.R. Co. — p. 157 ([scan leaf 936](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n936/mode/1up))

### Section XXIII. Exporters and Importers

**Chugai Boeki (Foreign and Domestic Trading) Co., K.K** Nakanoshima, Kitaku, Osaka, general exporters and importers. Est. 1917, cap. ¥2,000 (½ p.u.). Pres. M. Kita. Mng.-Dir. K. Sugiyama. — p. 157 ([scan leaf 936](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n936/mode/1up))


## The Japan Year Book

**D. Nagase & Co** Hirano-cho, Higashi-ku, Osaka, general exporters and importers; line of exports,—general Japanese produce, drugs, oils, chemicals, manufactured goods etc.; line of imports.—chemicals, dyes, drugs etc.; branches at Kobe, Kyoto and Tokyo, and London (65 Fenchurch St., E.C.) Pres. D. Nagase. — p. 158 ([scan leaf 937](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n937/mode/1up))

**E. H. Hunter & Co** Hirano-cho, Kobe. Formed 1868, by E. H. Hunter; first engaged in rice export business but have since begun to import machineries and also engaged in antimony refining business; have special connection with Osaka Iron Works. Prop. Ryutaro Hunter. — p. 158 ([scan leaf 937](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n937/mode/1up))

**F. Kanematsu & Co., K.K** Ito-machi, Kobe; est. 1888, exporters and importers of artificial fertilizers, textile fabrics, etc.; broking business in general. Cap. ¥4,000 p.u. Branches at Tokyo (1/1 Yuraku-cho, Koj.) Sydney, etc. Dirs. T. Kitamura, U. Mayeda, etc. — p. 158 ([scan leaf 937](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n937/mode/1up))

**Fujita-Gumi, Gm. K** Dojima, Osaka. Producers and exporters in electrolytic copper, electrolytic zinc, ferro alloys, etc. Est. 1893, as a private enterprise of Mr. Heitaro Fujita, the president. Dir. Hikosaburo, N. Saka. — p. 158 ([scan leaf 937](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n937/mode/1up))

**Furukawa Trading Co., K.K** —Vid. Sect. Mining. — p. 158 ([scan leaf 937](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n937/mode/1up))

**Gosho Goshi Kaisha, K.K** Nakanoshima, Osaka. Est. 1905. Cap. ¥25,000 (20,000 p.u.), res. ¥2,050 in '21; manufacturers and exporters of raw cotton, cotton yarn, underwear, etc., branches at Tsinglao Bombey, Shanghai, Calcutta, Tientsin, etc. Chairman, I. Abe, Mng.-Dir. S. Nose. — p. 158 ([scan leaf 937](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n937/mode/1up))

**Hamaguchi Trading Co., K.K** 3-chome, Koami-cho, Nihonbashi, Tokyo. Est. 1918; cap. ¥1,000 Pres. Kichiemon Hamaguchi. — p. 158 ([scan leaf 937](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n937/mode/1up))

**Horne Trading Co., K.K** Takiyama-cho, Kyobashi, Tokyo. Est. Oct. '18; cap. ¥600 p.u.,; chiefly deal in machinery, tools and material goods. Pres. T. G. Nee; Dirs. F. W. Horne, J. D. Lobb, etc.; Auds. E. W. S. Oustin, J. L. Kaufmann. — p. 158 ([scan leaf 937](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n937/mode/1up))

**Iwai Shoten, K.K** 4-chome, Kitahama, Osaka. Est. 1912; engaged in trading in general. cap. ¥10,000 (7,000 p.u.) Pres. K. Iwai, Mng.-Dir. Y. Yasuno. — p. 158 ([scan leaf 937](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n937/mode/1up))

**Kakiage Trading Co** Importers and exporters, Kiryu, Gummaken. Est. ¥1918; mfrs. and dealers in silk and cotton goods in general. Pres. B. Kakiage. — p. 158 ([scan leaf 937](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n937/mode/1up))

**Kawamata Kempu Seiran Kabushiki Kaisha** Masagocho, Yokohama. Leading silk exporters, est. '99 with capital ¥160 p.u. Brand Kawamata Habutaye, Deer brand. Dirs. K. Komatsu, K. Iwamura, G. Ninomiya, etc. — p. 158 ([scan leaf 937](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n937/mode/1up))

**Kodera & Co** Newchwang, Manchuria. Oil factory and shipping agents. Branches: London, Dairen, Kobe, Tokyo, Vladivostok, Mukden, Harbin, etc. Prop. Sukichi Kodera, Nakayamatedori, Kobe. — p. 158 ([scan leaf 937](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n937/mode/1up))

**Kuhara Trading Co., K.K** Nakanoshima, Osaka. Founded by Mr. Fusanosuke Kuhara, a leading merchant of Osaka; cap. ¥10,000 p.u. Pres. F. Kuhara. — p. 158 ([scan leaf 937](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n937/mode/1up))

**Meidi-ya, K.K** Importers and dealers in groceries, provisions and agents of Kirin Beer, Nunobiki Tansan Mineral Water, Buchanan's Whisky, J. Brown's Scotch Whisky, etc. Head Office, Honcho, Yoko- hama. Est. 1885 by late Mr. H. Isono; reorganized as partnership unlimited company 1903; changed into limited liability company May '11. Warranted purveyors to the Imperial Household and the Imperial Navy. Capital ¥500. Branches; Tokyo, Osaka, Kobe, Moji and Seoul. Directors: C. Isono (Pres.), M. Miyaji (Mng.-Dir.), K. Sano (Dir.) — p. 158 ([scan leaf 937](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n937/mode/1up))


## Business Directory

**Mitsui Bussan Kaisha, K.K** General importers and exporters. Surugacho, Nihombashi, Tokyo. One of the oldest establishments in this line. Reorganized in Oct. '09 as Joint Stock Co. with other enterprises, all practically owned by the 11 branches of the Mitsui family; capital ¥100,000 p.u. Mng.-Dirs. S. Watanabe, K. Fukui, S. Odagaki, M. Fujise. Branches: London, New York, &c. — p. 159 ([scan leaf 938](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n938/mode/1up))

**Naigai Kogyo Kaisha, K.K** Yuraku-cho, Kojimachi, Tokyo. Cap. ¥4,000. Imp'ters and exp'ters of machinery, motor-cars, building material. Pres. S. Wakao; Man.-Dir. T. Fujiwara; I. Mori, etc. — p. 159 ([scan leaf 938](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n938/mode/1up))

**Nichibei Silk Co., K.K** Yuraku-cho, Kojimachi, Tokyo. Est. 1919, with the object of exporting silk to U.S.A., etc. Capital ¥10,000 p.u. Mng.-Dir. S. Hoshino. — p. 159 ([scan leaf 938](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n938/mode/1up))

**Nippon Seicha Kaisha, (Japan Tea Firing Co.), K.K** general merchants and tea exporters, Motohama-cho, Yokohama. Founded Feb. 1894 with the object of exporting Japanese tea to other countries chiefly produced in Shizuoka; capital ¥200 p.u. Directors: K. Otani (Pres.) Konosuke Otani (Vice-Pres.) R. Nagai (Act. Mng.) — p. 159 ([scan leaf 938](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n938/mode/1up))

**Ogura & Co** Yamashita-cho, Yokohama (export dep't). Dealers in Manila hemp, China hemp, jute, fertilizers, straw and chip braid. Formosa hats, flax canvas, Manila rope, grain and seeds, etc. Head Office at Tokyo; branches at Osaka, Kobe, Manila, Hankow. — p. 159 ([scan leaf 938](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n938/mode/1up))

**Okura-gumi** See under Section General. — p. 159 ([scan leaf 938](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n938/mode/1up))

**Suzuka Firm** Importer, exporter and mfr. of fertilizers, reorganized as K.K. in '20; ¥50,000 fully paid up; 1-chome, Saga-cho, Fukagawa, Tokyo; connection world-wide. Pres. J. Suzuka. — p. 159 ([scan leaf 938](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n938/mode/1up))

**Tokyo Boeki (Foreign Trade) Co., K.K** Ginza, Kyobashi, Tokyo. Est. 1918 and engaged in the South Sea trade and reclamation work. Dirs. Y. Imanishi, S. Kawakami, etc. — p. 159 ([scan leaf 938](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n938/mode/1up))

**Uchida Trading Co., K.K** Naka-cho, Kobe. Est. 1917; cap. ¥5,000 p.u.; general exporters and importers. Pres. T. Yagi; Dirs. S. Uchida; T. Murakami, etc. — p. 159 ([scan leaf 938](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n938/mode/1up))

**Yano & Joko Co., [illegible] K** General exporters and importers; 76, Yamashita-cho, Yokohama. Speciality, waste silk; connection world-wide. — p. 159 ([scan leaf 938](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n938/mode/1up))

**Yonei & Co** General importers and exporters, Ginza, Tokyo. Est. in 1887; reorganized as K.K. in 1921; contractors to Government; agents for many foreign Cos.; Branches; Osaka, Kobe, Seoul, &c. Prop. N. Yonei. — p. 159 ([scan leaf 938](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n938/mode/1up))

**Yu-asa Firm** Takakura Higashi-iru, Gojo, Kyoto. Importers and exporters; dealers in metal wares, etc. Prop. Shichizaimon Yu-asa. — p. 159 ([scan leaf 938](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n938/mode/1up))

**Z. Horikoshi & Co** Tori-hatago-cho, Nih., Tokyo. General exporters and importers; est. 1893; mfrs. of and dealers in silks, silk and straw goods, pearl buttons, rugs, etc. Connection, worldwide. Pres. K. Horikoshi. — p. 159 ([scan leaf 938](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n938/mode/1up))


## Section XXIV. General

**Chuka (China) Enterprise Co., K.K** 1-chome, Yuraku-cho, Koj., Tokyo. Est. 1919; cap. ¥10,000; engaged in investment in and management of various enterprises in China; has a branch at Shanghai. Pres. S. Fujita. — p. 160 ([scan leaf 939](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n939/mode/1up))

**Japan-America Trust Co** Nakabashi-Izumicho, Kyobashi, Tokyo. Est. Oct. '18; cap. ¥50,000 subscribed by American and Japanese capitalists; engaged in investment in foreign countries, introduction of foreign capital, general broking and trust business; has a branch in Osaka. Pres. M. Sugawara, Mng.-Dir. K. Imanishi. — p. 160 ([scan leaf 939](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n939/mode/1up))

**Mitsubishi Firm, Ltd.; K.K** Carries on Shipbuilding, Banking, Mining, Iron Works, Trading, Warehousing and Insurance business each as independent company. Trading Co. as organized in 1918 is backed with capital ¥15,000, p.u. res. ¥2,900. Pres. Baron K. Iwasaki; Mng.-Dir. S. Sakamoto. — p. 160 ([scan leaf 939](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n939/mode/1up))

**Mitsui & Co., Gm. K** Surugacho, Nihombashi, Tokyo. Est. 1893 with cap. ¥60,000 in 1920. Controlling centre of all the undertakings run by the Mitsui. Pres. Baron Hachirojemon Mitsui. — p. 160 ([scan leaf 939](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n939/mode/1up))

**Nippon Shoji Kaisha (Japan Trading Co., Ltd.), K.K** Sueyoshitashi-dori, 2-chome, Minami-ku, Osaka and Kabutocho, Nihombashi-ku, Tokyo. Est. 1916, cap. ¥5,000 p.u.; general exporters and importers; Pres. Y. Kashima, Mng.-Dir. M. Sugimoto. — p. 160 ([scan leaf 939](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n939/mode/1up))

**Okuragumi, K.K** General exporters and importers, Ginza, Tokyo. The firm organized as a partnership with Baron Kihachiro Okura, well known merchant in the country, president, in 1873. Reorganized as a join stock Co. in Dec. '11: capital ¥4,000 p.u. increased to 10,000 later. In 1918 the mining and engineering dep'ts. were each formed into an independent company, the former with capital of ¥20,000 (Baron and Mr. Okuras, President and Vice-Pres. and J. Kadono (Mng.-Dir.) and the latter with capital of ¥2,000 and with T. Tokumi as managing director. — p. 160 ([scan leaf 939](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n939/mode/1up))

**Shosho Yoko, K.K** 3-chome, Ginza, Tokyo. Founded and presided by Mr. Tadasaburo Yamamoto, a parvenu shipowner; reorganized in 1917 as K.K., with cap. ¥2,500 p.u.; deal in carrying trade. — p. 160 ([scan leaf 939](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n939/mode/1up))

**Suzuki & Co., unltd** Kaigandori, Kobe. Est. 1887, capital ¥50,000 p.u. General merchants and manufacturers; Industrial Dep't.—camphor, fishoil, electrolytic cathode, copper, spelter, etc. Import Dep't.—sugar, grains, machineries, chemicals, etc. Export Dep't.—sugar, grains, metals, provisions, etc. Branches and Agencies at London, New York, Melbourne, Vladivostok, Shanghai, etc. Manager, S. Mori; Rep. of the Co., Mrs. Suzuki. — p. 160 ([scan leaf 939](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n939/mode/1up))

**Takata Shokai, Gm. K** Eiraku-cho, Marunouchi, Tokyo. General importers and exporters. Est. 1880, succeeding Mr. Bairs business, a German merchant; reorganized 1908, cap. ¥5,000. Contractors to Government and their connections are world wide. Branches: London, New York, Osaka and Shanghai. Pres. Kamakichi Takata, etc. — p. 160 ([scan leaf 939](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n939/mode/1up))


## Business Directory

**Yasuda Trading Co** Gofukucho, Nihombashi, Tokyo. Cap. ¥20,000 (5,838 p.u.) ; carries on various enterprises in which the Yasuda family is solely interested, those being Nail works in Tokyo and Edamitsu (Kyushu), Iron-works at Osaka, godown and transport business, etc. Pres. Zenjiro Yasuda. — p. 161 ([scan leaf 940](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n940/mode/1up))

Kikuchi (Mng.-Dir.) D. Takemura. — p. 161 ([scan leaf 940](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n940/mode/1up))

**Japan-China Bean Oil Mfg. Co., K.K** Shio-cho, Kyobashi, Tokyo. Est. 1907; cap. ¥3,000 p.u. Pres. K. Matsushita, Mng.-Dir. J. Furusawa. — p. 161 ([scan leaf 940](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n940/mode/1up))

**Japan Russia Industrial Co., K.K** 6/29 Kobikicho, Kyo., Tokyo. Est. '17; nominal cap. ¥10,000. Engaged in foreign trade in general, transportation, etc. Pres. O. Ichiki; Mng.-Dir. M. Kono. — p. 161 ([scan leaf 940](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n940/mode/1up))

**Karafuto (Saghalien) Industrial Co., K.K** Otomari, Saghalien. Est. 1914; cap. ¥11,000 (7,700 p.u.); Pres. H. Okawa. — p. 161 ([scan leaf 940](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n940/mode/1up))

**Kokusai (International) Motion Picture Co., K.K** 3-chome, Zaimoku-cho, Kyo., Tokyo. Organized in 1921; Cap. ¥10,000 (7,996 p.u.) Pres. K. Gata; Mng.-Dir. K. Tokunaga. — p. 161 ([scan leaf 940](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n940/mode/1up))

**Malay Rubber Plantation Co., K.K** Minami-Demma-cho, Kyo., Tokyo. Est. '12; cap. ¥3,000 (½ p.u.); is undertaking rubber planting at Malay Island, leasing land 2,890 acres. Directors: Chairman I. Wakita, Dirs. S. Hoshino, R. Ikeda. — p. 161 ([scan leaf 940](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n940/mode/1up))

**Manchuria Industrial Co., K.K** Mikawa-cho, Dairen. Est. 1917; cap. ¥1,250 p.u.; provide tenement-houses at Anshan-tien, South-Manchuria to clerks and workmen belonging to the newly founded iron foundry there. Pres. Z. Yasuda. — p. 161 ([scan leaf 940](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n940/mode/1up))

**Matsutake Cinema Co., K.K** Nakabashi, Izumi-cho, Kyob., Tokyo. Est. 1920. Pres. S. Shirai. — p. 161 ([scan leaf 940](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n940/mode/1up))

**Mitsubishi Warehousing Co., K.K** No. 7 Komatsu-cho, Fukagawa, Tokyo. The Tokyo Warehousing Co., est. 1916, assumed the present name in March '18 with extended business and increased cap- ital to ¥10,000 (½ p.u.); have branches at Kobe and Osaka. Pres. T Takeichi. — p. 161 ([scan leaf 940](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n940/mode/1up))

**Nippon Motion Picture Co** 1 Kami-Makicho, Nihom., Tokyo. Est. 1912; cap. ¥6,000 (3,375 p.u.) Pres. K. Fujita; Mng.-Dir. Y. Suzuki. — p. 162 ([scan leaf 941](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n941/mode/1up))

**Nippon Musical Instrument Mfg. Co., K.K** Shizuoka. Est. 1898; cap. ¥3,000. Instruments produced, as well as music, etc., are put to sale at the Kyoeki-sha, No. 14, Takekawa-cho, Kyobashi. Pres. Chiyomaru Amano. — p. 162 ([scan leaf 941](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n941/mode/1up))

**Nissho Trust Co., K.K** 1-chome, Minami-Hatcho-bori, Kyobashi, Tokyo. Est. '18; cap. ¥2,00 . Pres. S. Tanaka. — p. 162 ([scan leaf 941](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n941/mode/1up))

**Nitto Hosho Shintaku (Trust) Co., K.K** Minami-Demma-cho, Kyobashi, Tokyo. Est. 1918; cap. ¥2,000. Pres. B. Dazai. — p. 162 ([scan leaf 941](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n941/mode/1up))

**Odawara Electric Rly. Co., K.K** Odawara, Kanagawa Pref. Est. Feb. 1898. Cap. ¥3,300 p.u.; res. ¥31'. Pres. T. Nakane; Mng.-Dir. M. Honda. — p. 162 ([scan leaf 941](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n941/mode/1up))

**Oriental Exploitation Co., K.K** Uchisaiwai-cho, Koj., Tokyo. Est. July, 1909 in Korea under Government protection to guarantee profit of 6%; make investment in railway, mining, farming, engineering enterprise, etc.; cap. ¥50,000 p.u. ; seek the new sphere of activity in Manchuria, Mongolia, etc. Pres. E. Ishizuka; Dirs. N. Matsudaira, T. Kawakami. — p. 162 ([scan leaf 941](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n941/mode/1up))

**Osaka Electric Bulb Co., K.K** Nishinari-gun, Osaka. Est. 1907, cap. ¥1,250 p.u. Pres. S. Watanabe; Dir. K. Kimura. — p. 162 ([scan leaf 941](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n941/mode/1up))

**Osaka Mainichi Shimbun, K.K** Osaka, Est. 1883; reorganized lately as K.K.; cap. ¥1,250 (850 p.u.); run also the Tokyo Nichi-Nichi. Pres. H. Motoyama. — p. 162 ([scan leaf 941](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n941/mode/1up))

**Overseas Exploiting (Kaigai-kogyo) Co., K.K** No. 13, Sojuro-cho, Kyobashi, Tokyo. Est. 1918; cap. ¥2,150 p.u.; formed by amalgamation of the 4 emigration Cos., Tokyo, South American, Nitto, and Brazil. Pres. J. Koyama; Mng.-Dir. J. Shimoba. — p. 163 ([scan leaf 942](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n942/mode/1up))

**Taiwan Tea Mfg. and Colonization Co., K.K** Taihoku. Est. 1918; cap. ¥3,000; manufacturers of and dealers in oolong tea. Pres. K. Makoshi. — p. 163 ([scan leaf 942](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n942/mode/1up))

**Takeuchi & Co., Gs. K** Bakurocho, Nihombashi, Tokyo. Est. about 50 years ago: organized as Gs. K., 1911, cap. ¥300; one of the oldest makers of safes in Japan; capable to turn out 600 cases per month. — p. 163 ([scan leaf 942](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n942/mode/1up))

**Teikoku Cold Storage Co., K.K** No. 12 Akashicho, Kyobashi Tokyo. Est. 1912; cap ¥3,000 (½ p.u.,). Mng.-Dir. T. Takahashi. — p. 163 ([scan leaf 942](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n942/mode/1up))

**Toa Kogyo (Industrial) Co., K.K** Yayesu-cho, Tokyo. Est. 1893; cap. in 1921 ¥11,500 p.u.; supplies funds for undertakings in China. Dirs. S. Kadono, K. Shiraiwa, etc. — p. 163 ([scan leaf 942](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n942/mode/1up))

**Toa Tobacco Co., K.K** Minami-Nabecho, Kyobashi, Tokyo. Est. '06; cap. ¥5,800 p.u.; is carrying on the business in Chosen and Manchuria, and was transferred to the Government control in '21. — p. 163 ([scan leaf 942](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n942/mode/1up))

**Tokunaga Shoten** Yokoyamacho, Nihombashi, Tokyo. Manufacturers of and dealers in hair brushes; have a factory at Koishikawa, Shibuya and Tamagawa; annual output is estimated at ¥100. Prop Y. Tokunaga. — p. 163 ([scan leaf 942](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n942/mode/1up))

**Tokyo Cordage Mfg. Co., K.K** Kyobashi-ku, Tokyo. Est. 1897; cap. ¥10,000; (7,000 p.u.). Pres. M. Yamada; Mng.-Dir. N. Akamatsu. — p. 163 ([scan leaf 942](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n942/mode/1up))

**Tokyo Building Co., K.K** 1-chome, Gofuku-cho, Nihom., Tokyo. Est. 1896; cap. ¥10,000. Building contractors; loaners on and brokers in real estates; branches at Yokohama, Seoul, Tientsin, etc. Pres. Y. Yasuda, Vice-Pres. I. Yabuta; Mng.-Dir. H. Kitaki. — p. 163 ([scan leaf 942](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n942/mode/1up))

**Tokyo Hat Co., K.K** Hikawashita, Koishikawa, Tokyo. Est. 1906; cap. ¥1,000 (625 p.u.). Pres. Kyohei Makoshi. — p. 163 ([scan leaf 942](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n942/mode/1up))

**Tokyo Rubber Industry Co., K.K** Tori-1-chome, Nihomb., Tokyo. Est. 1917; cap. ¥1,587 p.u. Pres. K. Morimura; Dirs. T. Mayeda, etc. — p. 163 ([scan leaf 942](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n942/mode/1up))

**Tokyo Salvage Co., K.K** No. 1/1 Eiraku-cho, Koj., Tokyo. Est. 1917 with cap. ¥1,800; engaged broking in marine property, tow-boats, transportation, etc. Pres. T. Mogi; Mng.-Dir. S. Mori. — p. 163 ([scan leaf 942](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n942/mode/1up))

**Tokyo Taxi-Cab Co., K.K** 2-chome, Sukiya-cho, Nihom., Tokyo. Est. 1918; cap. ¥5,000 p.u.; intended for the present to run 180 taxies, 100 goods-waggons and 100 auto-cars on hire in Tokyo. Pres. K. Watanabe. Dirs. S. Wakao, R. Horiuchi, T. Suzuki, G. Abe, etc. — p. 163 ([scan leaf 942](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n942/mode/1up))

**Toshin Warehouse Co., K.K** Hakozaki-cho, Nihombashi. Tokyo. Est. in Oct. 1913. cap. ¥5,000, p.u. Dirs.; Chairman T. Mitsui; R. Kadono — p. 163 ([scan leaf 942](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n942/mode/1up))

**Yamamoto Tosuke & Co., K.K** 1-chome, Andoji-Dori, Minami- kn, Osaka. Est. 1918; cap. ¥2,000 p.u.; dealers in metal materials and hard wares. Pres. T. Yamamoto. — p. 163 ([scan leaf 942](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n942/mode/1up))


## The Japan Year Book

**Yokohama Nursery Co., K.K** Nakamura-cho, Yokohama. Est. 1906; exporters of nursery stocks, seed and plants; capital ¥500. Branches: London, New York, etc. Directors, U. Suzuki, S. Iida (Mng.) — p. 164 ([scan leaf 943](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n943/mode/1up))

**Yokohama Rubber Mfg. Co., K.K** Hiranuma, Y'hama. Est. in 1917 as a joint enterprise of Y'hama Wire Mfg. Co. and Goodrich Rubber Co., Ohio, each subscribing half of the total capital ¥2,500; sole agents for the American Co. in the Far East. Pres. Baron K. Nakajima; Dirs. K. Kawai, etc. — p. 164 ([scan leaf 943](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n943/mode/1up))

**Yuasa Storage Battery Co., K.K** Kyoto. The storage battery factory run by Mr. S. Yuasa (Pres.), enlarged and reorganized as such in 1918; cap. ¥5,000 (nominal). — p. 164 ([scan leaf 943](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n943/mode/1up))


## Learned & Social Institutions

### Section I Learned Institutions

**Agricultural Association, Japan** Founded 1881; publishes monthly organ Dai Nippon Nokai-ho, and maintains Tokyo Agricultural University (private) in Tokyo as its educational institution. Members number 38,000; dues; Spec. Mem. ¥30 in lump or ¥3 a year, Ord. Mem ¥24 in lump, or ¥2.40 a year. Pres. Marquis K. Matsudaira; Vice-Pres., Dr. A. Yokoi. Add. Tameike, Akasaka, Tokyo. — p. 165 ([scan leaf 944](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n944/mode/1up))

**Asiatic Society of Japan** Pioneer association of the kind in Japan and was est. about 1880 by the late Sir Earnest Satow and others in Japan for studying and investigating Japanese customs, history, literature, etc. Meeting held quarterly and proceedings published occasionally and distributed to members gratis. Hon. Secy. Earnest Wilson Clement. Add. Keio University, Tokyo. — p. 165 ([scan leaf 944](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n944/mode/1up))

**Association of Patent Attorneys** A foundation est. in April, 1922, as an advisory body in legal matters; members some 700 in July. Dirs. K. Inomata, S. Horii, etc. C/o Patent Bureau, Kojimachi, Tokyo. — p. 165 ([scan leaf 944](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n944/mode/1up))

**Bankers' Association, Tokyo** Founded 1900; fund consists of subscriptions in proportion to the capital paid up of the associated banks, ranging from ¥80 to ¥1,000 besides an admission fee of ¥200-1,000. The members number 88 banks. Pres., Y. Susaki (chairman), Vice-Pres., I. Matsukata & S. Ikeda. — p. 165 ([scan leaf 944](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n944/mode/1up))

**Barristers' Society, Japan** Founded June, 1896; issues a monthly periodical; members number some 3,300. Dues; 30 sen for ordinary members per month. Dirs., C. Iwata, K. Ishii, S. Uzawa, etc. Add. Nishi-Hibiya, Kojimachi, Tokyo. — p. 165 ([scan leaf 944](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n944/mode/1up))

**Botanical Society, Japan** Founded 1882; publishes a monthly Botanical Magazine. Dues ¥6 per annum. Pres., Dr. J. Matsumura; Mng.-Dir.; Dr. K. Shibata. Add. Botanical Garden, Tokyo. — p. 165 ([scan leaf 944](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n944/mode/1up))

**Carnegie Endowment for International Peace, Division of Economics and History, Japanese Committee** Est. in July, 1912 in accordance with the International Conference of Economists held in 1911 in Berne. The result of investigation is published at the head office in New York, U.S.A. President, Baron Y. Sakatani. Secy. Y. Yamamoto. Office at Senshu Daigaku, Kanda, Tokyo. — p. 165 ([scan leaf 944](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n944/mode/1up))


## The Japan Year Book

**Chemical Society, Nippon** Founded 1878; dues for a regular member ¥10 per annum, associate ¥7, those numbering 1,000 in all. Publishes a monthly bulletin. Add. Science College, Imp. University, Tokyo. — p. 166 ([scan leaf 945](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n945/mode/1up))

**Electric Association, Japan** Founded 1912; subscription ¥6 a year. Chief Secy., K. Uyeno. Add. Sanchome, Yurakucho, Kojimachi, Tokyo. — p. 166 ([scan leaf 945](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n945/mode/1up))

**Emigration Society, Japan** Founded 1914; subscription ¥10 per annum. Members number 5,000. Vice-Pres., J. Soyeda. Add. Kojimachi, Tokyo. — p. 166 ([scan leaf 945](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n945/mode/1up))

**Esperanto Society, Tokyo** Mem. 750; dues, ¥2 per annum. Representative Mem., M. Takahata. Add. 3/14 Shin-Ogawa-machi, Ushigome, Tokyo. — p. 166 ([scan leaf 945](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n945/mode/1up))

**Fishery Association, Japan** —Founded in 1882 and reorganized into a corporation in '09. Dues, ¥1 for ordinary Mem. Pres., B. Maki; Mng.-Dir., K. Shimo. Add. 1 Tameike, Akasaka, Tokyo. — p. 166 ([scan leaf 945](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n945/mode/1up))

**Foreign Trade Association, Japan** Founded 1886; members 500 in all. Representatives, K. Ikeda, K. Otani, O. Suzuki; Secy. T. Ikeda. Add 7. Sojutrocho, Kyobashi, Tokyo. — p. 166 ([scan leaf 945](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n945/mode/1up))

**Forestry Association, Japan** Founded in 1915 as a corporation; hold lecture meetings; publishes books and reports. Members 7,500; dues ¥3 a year. Chairman, Z. Hawase. Add. 1 Tameike, Akasaka, Tokyo. — p. 166 ([scan leaf 945](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n945/mode/1up))

**Geographical Society, Tokyo** Founded 1879; number of members 350; dues ¥9 a year. Patron, Prince Kan-in; Pres. Count R. Matsudaira. Add. 19 Nishi Konya-cho, Kyobashi, Tokyo. — p. 166 ([scan leaf 945](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n945/mode/1up))

**Historical Society** Members, 1,000; dues ¥4 a year. Pres. Dr. K. Tsuboi. Add. Historical Compilation Committee, Imp. Tokyo Univ. — p. 166 ([scan leaf 945](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n945/mode/1up))

**Horticulture Society, Japan** Founded 1899; publishes a monthly magazine. Vice-Pres., Viscount T. Fujinami. Add. 696 Nakashibuya, Tokyo. — p. 166 ([scan leaf 945](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n945/mode/1up))

**Imperial Academy** Founded in 1879 and now subject to control of Minister of Education; joined 1906 the International Association of Academies. Members limited to 60 elected from among learned scholars of literature and humanity (1st Dept.), and science pure and applied (2nd Dept.), and are nom. by the Emperor. Pres. is elected by mutual vote for a term of 3 years; undertakes translation and research works; publishes proceedings. Pres. Dr. Baron C. Hozumi; Manager, Dr. J. Sakurai. Loc. Uyeno Park, Tokyo. — p. 166 ([scan leaf 945](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n945/mode/1up))

**Imperial Academy of Arts** (Vide Chapter on Arts and Crafts, Y.Y.B.) — p. 166 ([scan leaf 945](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n945/mode/1up))

**Imperial Aviation Association** Founded 1914; member 5,000. Dues, regular member ¥100 in lump or annually ¥12 for ten years, common member ¥10 or ¥3 annually for five years, was admitted into the International Aviation Convention in 1919. Patron, H.H. Gen. Prince Kuni. Vice-Pres., Baron Y. Sakatani. Add. Mitsubishi Building, Marunouchi, Tokyo. — p. 166 ([scan leaf 945](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n945/mode/1up))


## Learned & Social Institutions

**Imperial Railway Association** Founded 1898; publishes as organ Teikoku Tetsudo Kyokai Kwaiho; members 2,300 in all. Honorary members confined to those who have rendered distinguished services to railway business. Rep. mem., Dr. K. Nomura. Add. Marunouchi, Tokyo. — p. 167 ([scan leaf 946](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n946/mode/1up))

**Industrial Society, Japan** Founded 1906; publishes a monthly journal; members numbered 1,181 in 1922; Pres., K. Uchida. Add. 1/1 Yamashitacho, Kyobashi, Tokyo. — p. 167 ([scan leaf 946](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n946/mode/1up))

**Jurisprudence Society** Founded 1894; annual due ¥2; members some 300 at present. Pres., Dr. Baron Chincho Hozumi; Dirs., T. Minobe, G. Suehiro. Add. c/o Hozumi, Hamikata-machi, Ushigome, Tokyo. — p. 167 ([scan leaf 946](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n946/mode/1up))

**Jurisprudence Society, Kyoto** Organized by University men, Kyoto. Add. Law Coll., Imp. Kyoto Univ. — p. 167 ([scan leaf 946](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n946/mode/1up))

**Keimei-kai** A foundation est. in 1918 with a fund of ¥1,000,000 contributed by Mr. Tetsuma Akaboshi, a millionaire, for facilitating special researches, investigations, compilations and invention; translates foreign works; holds lecture meetings; issues publications, etc. Dirs., N. Hirayama (Chairman), S. Tsurumi (Mng.-Dir.), M. Kushida, I Yamanouchi, etc. — p. 167 ([scan leaf 946](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n946/mode/1up))

**Kiitsu Kyokai** Organized about 1900 by leading thinkers and religionists for discussing problems of religion and thoughts. Pres., Dr. Baron Y. Sakatani. Add. 117 Hakusan-Gotenmachi, Koishikawa, Tokyo. — p. 167 ([scan leaf 946](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n946/mode/1up))

**Librarian's Association, Japan** Est. in 1892; reorganized in '06 for investigating matters concerning library management, etc.; publishes reports; members total 1,021. Pres., Marq. Y. Tokugawa. Add. 32 Gazembo, Azabu, Tokyo. — p. 167 ([scan leaf 946](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n946/mode/1up))

**Life Assurance Society** Founded 1908; publishes a quarterly bulletin; subscription to be levied in proportion to business result of the Cos. Dirs. Y. Fukuhara, T. Yano, D. Shimogo, etc. Add. Yuraku-cho, Kojimachi, Tokyo. — p. 167 ([scan leaf 946](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n946/mode/1up))

**Meteorological Society, Japan** Founded 1882; publishes monthly magazine. Dues, ordinary mem. ¥4 per annum; members number 340. Pres., Dr. S. Nakamura; Chief Secy., T. Okada. Add. Meteorological Observatory, Tokyo. — p. 167 ([scan leaf 946](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n946/mode/1up))

**Minakata Botanical Laboratory** Organized in 1922 for supporting Mr. Kumaku Minakata a noted scholar in his line for helping his researches in moss and lichens; fund ¥100,000; publishes reports. Loc. Tanabe, Wakayama-ken. — p. 167 ([scan leaf 946](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n946/mode/1up))

**Mining Industry Society, Japan** Founded 1885; publishes a magazine as its organ. Dues, ordinary mem. ¥10 annually and ¥5 for admission; supporting member is asked to contribute ¥300 or upward in lump; members 1,600 in all. Dir., M. Oi. Add. Kagacho, Kyobashi, Tokyo. — p. 167 ([scan leaf 946](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n946/mode/1up))


## The Japan Year Book

**National Research Council** As gazetted on Nov. 26, 1920 it consists of 2 Depts, the General Affairs and Scientific; has in maximum 190 scientists as its members. It comprises 8 branches of investigation, physics, chemistry, geography, biology, medicine, engineering, astronomy and geophysics, and will cooperate with the International Research Council formed by the late Allied States. It will also serve as a coordinating medium for various scientific associations at home. The first general meeting was held in Dec. 1920. Pres. Dr. Baron K. Furuichi. Vice-Pres. Dr. J. Sakurai. — p. 168 ([scan leaf 947](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n947/mode/1up))

**National Society of Medicine, (Kokka-Igaku-kai)** Members, 3,500; dues, ¥4.80. Add. Medical Jurisprudence Dept., Imp. Tokyo Univ. — p. 168 ([scan leaf 947](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n947/mode/1up))

**Oriental Association (formerly called Taiwan Kyokwai)** founded 1898, for developing resources in Formosa, Korea and Manchuria. Has established schools in Tokyo, Seoul, Dairen, etc. bringing up students to devote themselves to colonization work. Subscription ¥6 per annum for ordinary member; issues monthly magazine, Toyo Jiho; members number 3,500. Pres. E. Komatsubara, ex-Minister of Education; Vice-Pres., Baron K. Okura; Dirs. M. Kadono, etc. Add. Marunouchi, Tokyo. — p. 168 ([scan leaf 947](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n947/mode/1up))

**Philosophical Society, Kyoto** Organized by the professors and graduates of Philosophical Course, Imp. Kyoto Univ.; dues ¥4.40 per annum. Add. Kyoto. — p. 168 ([scan leaf 947](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n947/mode/1up))

**Scientific and Chemical Institute** A foundation established in 1917 under Govt. encouragement; funds ¥2,500,000 in May, 1922, then 10[illegible] students conducting researches and 13 studying abroad. Gen.-Dir., Dr. Vise. M. Okochi. Loc. Kami-Fujimaye, Hongo, Tokyo. — p. 168 ([scan leaf 947](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n947/mode/1up))

**Sericultural Society, Japan** Founded 1892; publishes a Japanese monthly and a French Bulletin as its organ. Reliable information is supplied on application to foreigners abroad. Dues, for special member ¥50 in lump, and for ordinary ¥3; members number 227,000. Patron, Prince Kan-in; Pres. Viscount T. Makino; Vice-Pres., G. Shimura. Add. 2/1 Yuraku-cho, Kojimachi, Tokyo. — p. 168 ([scan leaf 947](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n947/mode/1up))

**Society for the Propagation of Roman Alphabets** Publishes its organ in Roman letters; members 3,000; dues, ¥1 per annum. Add. No. 3 1-chome, Yuraku-cho, Kojimachi, Tokyo. — p. 168 ([scan leaf 947](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n947/mode/1up))

**Society for Promoting Scientific Knowledge** Organized in Jan. 1921; publishes its organ, "Kagaku-chishiki"; holds lecture meetings. Add. 1/3 Uchisaiwai-cho, Kojimachi, Tokyo. — p. 168 ([scan leaf 947](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n947/mode/1up))

**Statiscal Society, Tokyo** Founded 1873; publishes monthly magazine styled Tokei Shushi, and also issues the Imperial Japanese Statistic Annual and a number of statistics. Dues, ¥6 a year; have membership of 863. Pres., Dr. Baron Y. Sakatani. Add. Yamashiro-cho, Kyobashi, Tokyo. — p. 168 ([scan leaf 947](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n947/mode/1up))

**Teikoku Hatsumei Kyokai (Society for Protecting Industrial Property)** A corporation founded in 1905; members 2,664 in 1922; dues ¥100 and over in lump or ¥6 a year for ordinary mem... ¥200 and over in Imp or ¥12 a year for special mem. Pres. Baron Y. Sakatani; Vice-Pres. T. Dan, K. Otani, and K. Kume. Add. 1/1, Yuraku-cho, Kojimachi, Tokyo. — p. 168 ([scan leaf 947](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n947/mode/1up))


## Learned & Social Institutions

**Teikoku Kyoikukai (Imp. Educational Society)** —Est. in 1882; an unofficial advisory body in educational matters; combines training of teachers for secondary schools; has its members all over the country; publishes books and magazines; awards honor to those of meritorious services to education; fund ¥344,000. Pres. Dr. M. Sawayanagi. Loc. 21 Hitotsubashi, Kanda, Tokyo. — p. 169 ([scan leaf 948](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n948/mode/1up))

**University Graduates' Society** —Founded 1886; admits graduates of Imperial Universities of Tokyo, Kyoto, Kyushu, Tohoku and Hokkaido, and also others who have connections with the above institutions. Dues, ¥4 per annum, or ¥40 for life; publishes monthly bulletin; comprises 18 committees and 1 chief secretary. Add. Nishikicho, Kanda, Tokyo. — p. 169 ([scan leaf 948](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n948/mode/1up))

**Zoological Society, Tokyo** —Founded 1879; annual subscription ¥12 for class "A" member, ¥6, for "B" member; members total 3[illegible]6; the Zoological Magazine is issued monthly, with "Annotations Zoological Japonensis" as occasional publication. Pres., Prof. S. Goto (c/o Imp. Univ. Tokyo); Secy. Dr. N. Yatsu. — p. 169 ([scan leaf 948](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n948/mode/1up))

### Social Institutions

**Belgo-Japanese Association** —Founded 1889; members number about 74 comprising only those who have studied in Belgium. Secy., Mr. S. Akasaka. 53 Fujimicho, Azabu, Tokyo. — p. 169 ([scan leaf 948](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n948/mode/1up))

**British Society** —Found 1908 by the late Count Hayashi, the late Sir Claude MacDonald, Count Hirosawa, the late Capt. Brinkley, the late Viscount Inaba, the late Vice-Admiral Baron Miyabara, Count Kawamura, etc.; dues ¥5 a year for ordinary members or ¥50 in one sum for life. The membership numbers about 250. Add. British Embassy, Tokyo. — p. 169 ([scan leaf 948](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n948/mode/1up))

**Eiraku Club** —Formed in 1914 by graduates and friends of Waseda University as social organ. Membership about 300; dues ¥ 36 a year; one half for country member; Pres. Dr. S. Takata. Sec. S. Tamura. Add. Eiraku-cho, Tokyo. Tel. Central 326. — p. 169 ([scan leaf 948](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n948/mode/1up))

**Franco-Japanese Society** —Founded 1886. List of members numbers over 300 including five grades of members; dues of ordinary member ¥3.60 a year. Patrons, Prince Nashimoto and French Ambassador; Chief Dir. Baron K. Furuichi, Dr. Eng. Add. Hibiya Park, Tokyo. — p. 169 ([scan leaf 948](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n948/mode/1up))

**Indo-Japanese Association** —Founded 1902; covers British India, French Indo-China, Dutch islands in the South Seas. Members 570 in all. Vice-Pres., Baron N. Kanda, Dr. F. Nanjo; Secy. Y. Soyejima. Add. 36 Akashi-cho, Kyobashi, Tokyo. — p. 169 ([scan leaf 948](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n948/mode/1up))

**Industrial Club, Japan** —A corporation founded in 1917 by manufacturers for investigating various industrial problems, encouraging invention, promoting industry, etc. Dues ¥60 for city members and ¥30 for country members besides ¥1,000 of an admission fee. Members total 710. Chairman, T Dan. Mng.-Dir., S. Ohashi, T. Wada, etc. Loc., Yurakucho, Koj., Tokyo. — p. 169 ([scan leaf 948](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n948/mode/1up))


## The Japan Year Book

**International Peace Association** Est. in 1914 "for breaking down prejudices and antipathy between nations for the sake of justice and humanity and for contributing to world's peace and welfare, etc." Dues ¥6 a year. Dirs., Dr. M. Niida, J. Okabe, G. Kasuya, N. Seki, etc. Add. 1/3 Uchisaiwai-cho, Kojimachi, Tokyo. — p. 170 ([scan leaf 949](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n949/mode/1up))

**Kojunsha** Founded 1879 by graduates of and others instructed in Keio University and is social club. Entrance fee ¥300; annual subscription ¥36. Membership over 1,000. Pres., E. Kamada; Chief Dirs., J. Kudono, H. Ishikawa. Add. Nabecho Nichome, Kyobashi, Tokyo. — p. 170 ([scan leaf 949](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n949/mode/1up))

**Latin American-Japanese Society** Created by Japanese interested in Central and South Americas, or peoples of these republics connected with this country, about 120 in number. Add. Hibiya Park, Tokyo. — p. 170 ([scan leaf 949](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n949/mode/1up))

**Nippon Club** Est. 1888 by Japanese; is a non-political institution. List of members about 500 including country members; entrance fee ¥30, annual subscription ¥36. The present building was built in 1906. Pres. Viscount C. Okabe. Add. Yurakucho, Kojimachi, Tokyo. — p. 170 ([scan leaf 949](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n949/mode/1up))

**Russo-Japanese Society** Founded 1906; members number 350; dues, ¥6 a year for ordinary members or ¥200 for life and ¥500 for special members. Pres., Visc. S. Goto. Add. Uchisaiwaicho, Marunouchi, Tokyo. — p. 170 ([scan leaf 949](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n949/mode/1up))

**Toa-Dobun-Kai** The Toa-kai and Dobun-kai originally organized by those interested in Chinese affairs, amalgamated under the present title in 1868; reorganized into a corporation in 1922; promotes friendly relations between Japan and China; consists of two Depts., Education and Investigation & Compilation. Maintains schools in Shanghai, Tientsin, Hankow and Tokyo for educating Chinese and Japanese. Members' roll contains 2,680. Pres. Visc. N. Makino; Vice-Pres., Prince Konoye; Chief Secretary, R. Shimaiwa. Add. 2 Tameike, Akasaka, Tokyo. — p. 170 ([scan leaf 949](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n949/mode/1up))

**Tokyo Club** Est. 1885 by Japanese and foreigners; entrance fee ¥200; annual subscription ¥120 for city members and ¥36 for country members. List of members about 450. The present building was built in 1908 at the cost of ¥300,000. Pres. H.H. Prince Kan-in; Vice-Pres., British Ambassador, Marquis K. Inouye, Hon. Secy., Mr. A. W. Medley; Hon. Treas., Viscount H. Akimoto. Add. Sannen-cho, Kojimachi, Tokyo. Tel. No. 3021-4 Ginza. — p. 170 ([scan leaf 949](https://archive.org/details/japan-year-book-1923/page/n949/mode/1up))
